Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with this. 

Well, I guess... it's probably just a small thing, really. Nothing too serious, I hope. Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I like being around people! I don't avoid socializing or anything like that. I don't really worry about people judging me... at least, not any more than the average person does, right?

I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right kind of information to start with.\n\nDoctor: Do you also have trouble hearing people in other situations, like in noisy rooms or when someone speaks from another room?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, actually. My hearing itself seems to be perfectly fine, thankfully. Even if it's noisy or someone is calling from the kitchen, I can hear the sounds without any trouble. \n\nI actually went and had my ears checked a little while ago, just to make sure I wasn't making a fuss over nothing, you know? I didn't want to waste anyone's time if all I needed was a hearing aid. But the doctor there said my hearing is great for a guy my age. So it's not the volume... it's just that the words sort of slip away from me even when it's quiet and they're right in front of me. \n\nI hope I'm explaining that okay? I know you have to ask about all the possibilities, so I hope that helps narrow it down a little.\n\nDoctor: When someone is speaking to you, do you understand the words themselves, or do they sometimes sound like gibberish?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not gibberish. I understand the words just fine, thankfully. I wouldn't want you to think I'm, you know, completely losing my grip on things! \n\nThe words make perfect sense when people are saying them. It's really just... well, my attention just sort of takes a little detour, I suppose. I hear the words, and I know exactly what they mean, but then I just realize I wasn't really absorbing the whole thought. \n\nDoes that answer what you were asking? I just want to make sure I'm doing this right and giving you the details you actually need.\n\nDoctor: When you read written text, do you ever have trouble understanding what the words mean?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, no trouble like that at all. Reading is... well, it's perfectly fine. I know I only went through high school, but I've always understood what I'm reading. It's really just that little focus thing I mentioned when people are talking to me. But reading a book or, you know, a placard down at the museum... no, I understand the meaning of the written words just fine. \n\nIs that what you were looking for? I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions the right way and not leading you down the wrong path.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble understanding what people say to you, even when you can hear them clearly?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, not at all. I think... well, it's a lot like what I was trying to say a moment ago, though maybe I didn't do the best job explaining it! I understand what people are telling me just fine. The meaning is all there, and I definitely don't have any trouble grasping what they're trying to communicate to me. \n\nIt's really just that little issue of my attention drifting off, rather than not actually comprehending them. I hope I'm not making this more complicated than it needs to be? I just want to be sure I'm giving you exactly what you need to know and not confusing the issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have trouble finding the right words when you’re speaking?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. Finding words is... well, it's usually pretty easy for me. I can hold a conversation just fine, and I don't really find myself grasping for what to say or anything like that. \n\nI mean, everyone forgets a name or a specific word every now and then, right? But nothing out of the ordinary. I really don't want you to think I'm having trouble communicating or, you know, losing my vocabulary! I'm pretty articulate, I think. \n\nDoes that help clear that up? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture of how things are going.\n\nDoctor: When someone speaks to you, do you ever have trouble understanding what the words mean?\n\nPatient: No, no, I understand the words perfectly. It's really just like I was saying before... the words themselves make total sense. I just, you know, sometimes my mind wanders off while they're talking. \n\nBut I definitely know what the words mean! I'm not having any trouble with my hearing or my understanding, I promise. I really hope I'm making sense and not just repeating myself too much. I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right information. Is this all helpful for you?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told you have trouble hearing, or needed a hearing test before this started?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not. My hearing is actually quite good, I think. I've never had anyone tell me I couldn't hear them, and I haven't ever needed to go in for a hearing test or anything like that. \n\nI'm pretty sure everything is working just fine in that department! It really all just comes back to the attention thing, not my ears. I hope that's the right kind of answer you're looking for? I just want to be absolutely sure I'm not leading you down the wrong path with all this.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid social situations because you fear being judged or embarrassed by other people?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say that. I just sort of... drift a bit. But I still go out and see folks, and I enjoy it. I really hope that doesn't make me sound like a hermit or anything! I'm pretty normal when it comes to being around others, I think. I just try to be polite and pleasant. I hope that's the kind of thing you're asking? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right picture here.\n\nDoctor: Do you avoid speaking up in groups because you’re afraid of being judged or embarrassed?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I avoid speaking up. I just try to be a good listener when I'm in a group.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever forget appointments, errands, or conversations in your day-to-day life?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I don't think so. Oh, um, no, not at all. I mean, I think I'm pretty comfortable around folks. I don't really worry about them thinking poorly of me or anything like that. 

I just try to be friendly and treat everyone with respect, and I figure if I do that, there's no reason for anyone to judge me, right? I mean, maybe sometimes I worry a tiny bit if I've been quiet for a while because I lost track of the conversation... I just don't want anyone to think I'm being rude or ignoring them. But it's not a strong fear or anything. Just wanting to be a good neighbor, really. Is that the kind of thing you're looking for? I hope I'm answering these questions okay for you.

No, I don't think so. I mean, you just asked something similar, right? I hope I didn't confuse you with my last answer. But no, I don't feel a strong fear of being embarrassed or judged.

Oh, goodness, no. No, I don't get lost. I've lived around here for years, so I know my way around pretty well.

If I need to run to the bank or pay for something at the hardware store, I don't have any trouble. Sometimes I might take a second longer to double-check my change, just to be careful, but I've never overdrawn the account or forgotten to pay the mortgage or anything terrible like that. We're doing okay on our retirement savings, and I make sure we don't spend more than we should. I try to be really responsible with our money. That's a good thing, right? I'm not doing too bad for a guy my age, am I?
